Dear Ms Reid,


  Opportunity to make submissions about a proposed Banning Order under section 73ZN of the
                     National Disability Insurance Scheme Act 2013 (Cth)


I am a delegate of the Commissioner for the NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission (the
Commission).

Section 73ZN(2) of the National Disability Insurance Scheme Act 2013 (Cth) (NDIS Act) gives me, as the
Commissioner’s delegate, the power to make an order known as a Banning Order. A Banning Order
can prohibit or restrict a person who is or was employed or otherwise engaged by an NDIS provider
under the National Disability Insurance Scheme (NDIS) from engaging in specified activities.

Proposed Banning Order

I have formed the preliminary view that it may be appropriate in all the circumstances to make a
Banning Order prohibiting you for a period of ten (10) years from being involved in the provision of
supports or services to people with disability. However, should a banning order be made it will not
apply in relation to any supports or services that you may seek for yourself or that you may provide to
your son.

The basis for this preliminary view is that I reasonably believe you have contravened, are contravening,
or are likely to contravene the NDIS Act, as referred to in section 73ZN(2)(a)(i) of the NDIS Act. Please
refer to Annexure A for more information.

Opportunity to make submissions

The purpose of this letter is to inform you of relevant facts and the matters I have considered in
reaching my preliminary view and to give you the opportunity to make a submission about this before
I make a final decision.

I will not make a final decision whether to make a Banning Order until you have had an opportunity
to make submissions to me. This gives you an opportunity to tell me anything that you believe may be
appropriate to bring to my attention, to dispute any of the facts of this matter, to comment on the
seriousness of it or to put your view as to the terms of any Banning Order that I may make.




                                  NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission                               1
                                                199
                                                                                                   T9.7

Relevant facts and information

As a result of information provided to the Commission and the Commission’s own enquiries, I
understand the following:

   1. You are a Director and one of the key personnel of Forward Focused Group Pty Ltd, ABN 98
      643 878 639 (Forward Focused Group).

   2. Forward Focused Group Pty Ltd is a registered NDIS provider with registration ID 4-4GHG-
      1073.

   3. According to information available to the Commission, you have assigned yourself with
      authority to make decisions on behalf of NDIS participants. This information suggests that you
      are not taking all reasonable steps to prevent and respond to all forms of exploitation of
      people with disability. By way of example,

           a. The Commission holds a record of a Forward Focused Group document titled
              ‘FM Consent Third Party to Act’ which provides ongoing consent for any Forward
              Focused staff member (contact Vida Reid), which includes consent to request review
              of NDIA decisions and manage complaints made by participant Meghan Goodwin.

   4. The Commission has received information and allegations that strongly suggest that you
      frequently engage in bullying, harassment and intimidation behaviours directed at NDIS
      participants and Forward Focused Group staff. Your alleged conduct towards participants and
      staff have created an environment whereby participants and staff do not feel supported or
      safe to raise any concerns they have about the quality and safety of supports provided by
      Forward Focused Group.

   5. Since September 2021, the Commission has received over 30 complaints regarding the
      safety and quality of supports provided by Forward Focused Group. The Commission holds
      information that suggests that you, as a representative of the Provider, and the Provider do
      not support individuals to raise any complaints they may have directly with the provider,
      without fear of retaliation from yourself or Forward Focused Group. This information
      strongly indicates that you, as a director and key personnel, are not ensuring that complaints
      are welcomed, acknowledged, respected and well-managed.

   6. The Commission also holds information that you are not acting with respect for individual
      rights to freedom of expression, self-determination and decision-making in accordance with
      applicable laws and conventions. Across June and July 2022, participants including Corina
      Abraham, Sarah-Jane Clulow, and Alexsis Bott all sort to amend or cease service agreements
      with the Provider. Your email correspondence indicated non-compliance with the National
      Disability Insurance Scheme (Code of Conduct) Rules 2018 and included both veiled and
      direct threats, bullying and harassment. By way of example,

           •   Across May and June 2022, the Provider attempted to obtain control of participant
               funds and participants supports for participant Alexsis Bott. When the participant
               confirmed, in writing that they didn’t want to take the action to release their funds
               from the existing manager you, as key personnel, write “we will be terminating your
               services with us effective immediately”.




                                 NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission                            2
                                               200
                                                                                                       T9.7

       •   Series of emails 24 June 2022 to 19 June 2022 regarding participant Sarah-Jane
           Clulow advising a four week notice for service termination. The emails show that the
           participant was seeking review of an invoice for services not received.

       •   On 1 July 2022, you, as key personnel, respond to an account statement from a
           supplier stating that, ““we will no longer pay any invoices for Sarah. She has
           terminated her contract with us.”

       •   Series of emails 15 June 2022 to 19 June 2022 regarding participant Corina Abraham
           ceasing some services with Hybrid Hive. The participant seeks to meet to discuss
           their concerns. By email dated 19 June, you, as key personnel, purport to cease
           support coordination and terminate the contract for all services with immediate
           effect. The email claims “ongoing abusive manner of our staff by yourself and the
           break in trust” as the grounds. The email includes a perceived threat that invoices
           are to be paid immediately or be reported to the NDIS.

       •   Email 16 June 2022 sent by you to participant, Ms Abraham, provides that the
           support worker has a non-compete and non-disclosure contract which will prevent
           her for working privately for the participant. It also states that if the participant had
           “come to us in good faith…I would have said okay.”

       •   Email 16 June 2022 sent by you to participant, Ms Abraham, “I would like all of your
           outstanding invoices paid immediately please or I will report this to the NDIS”.

7. The Commission holds additional information that indicates that you have played a central
   role in the alleged fraudulent activities engaged in by Forward Focused Group, in particular
   the submission of false claims for payments to the National Disability Insurance Scheme
   (Scheme). I am of the view that this demonstrates a failure by you to act with integrity,
   honesty and transparency.

   It is alleged that Forward Focused Group are knowingly submitting false claims for payments
   to the National Disability Insurance Scheme (Scheme), with the intention of dishonestly
   causing a loss to another person, namely the NDIA, a Commonwealth entity. The
   Commission is aware of questions about invoices raised against multiple participants.

   The Commission holds information about participants Tiffany Rose and Andrew Hahn on
   dates known between 26 November 2021 and 14 June 2022, which show that the Provider
   generated invoices that did not match the services provided, or were generated without the
   necessary Plans or Services Agreements in place.

   The Commission also holds information, through emails dated 3 May and 9 May 2022, which
   show that you are withholding information from other staff of the Provider on potentially
   fraudulent invoices. By way of example,

       a. Internal email dated 3 May 2022 from former employee to the Chief Financial
          Officer raising questions regarding an invoice for Support Coordination services for
          participant that Forward Focused Group had not provided. In your response dated 9
          May 2022, you replied “Whilst I appreciate your thoroughness in dealing with clients
          please remember that I do work on participants as well. I keep my work and my
          notes private as sometimes they may be about staff or complaints or other work
          clients have requested that they have advised not to be disclosed to other due to
          privacy and dignity.”….“I am aware that you have recently gained access to some



                             NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission                                    3
                                           201
                                                                                                          T9.7

                 portals and kindly ask that you do not access these as they go against our policy and
                 procedures.”

        The Commission holds records of correspondence between yourself and participants which
        reflects a lack of integrity and honesty in dealings including accusations of non-payment or
        broken trust as grounds for you to immediately terminate engagements with participants.
        By way of example,

            a. Termination Notice Letter from DFG Legal WA dated 31 March 2022 requesting that
               participant Andrew Hahn vacate a Forward Focused Group-managed property due
               to “serious misconduct” and “failing to pay the fortnightly lodging fee”. The
               Commission holds a record of an email from the advocate of the participant dated
               27 March 2022, requesting that “unsubstantiated threats of eviction” from the
               Provider cease.

Proposed Banning Order

The information available to the Commission indicates that you engage in dishonest and intimidating
conduct that falls below the standard expected from key personnel of a registered NDIS provider and
the director of a company that provides disability supports and services.

The need to protect the interests of people with disability, who may be particularly vulnerable, and
ensure they are kept safe, outweighs your interest in continuing to be involved in the provision of
supports or services to people with a disability.

Therefore, my preliminary view is that I should make a Banning Order that prohibits you from being
involved in the provision of supports or services to people with disability for a period of ten (10) years,
on the basis that you have contravened, are contravening, or are likely to contravene the NDIS Act, as
per section 73ZN(2)(a)(i) of the NDIS Act.

I am of the view that making a Banning Order against you would provide a strong level of protection
for people with disability and maintain community confidence in the Scheme.

In forming this preliminary view, I have placed significant weight on the following matters:

    •   The nature of your alleged breaches of the NDIS Act are serious, as they involve instances of
        verbal and emotional abuse involving threats towards vulnerable persons with disability to
        whom you were providing services and supports on a regular basis. The Commission holds
        information that suggested that you exerted undue influence over a number of participants
        that limited them from exercising their choice and control over their own supports and
        services;

    •   I am particularly concerned by recent reports to the Commission that indicate your breaches
        of the NDIS Act are still occurring. The Commission holds information that suggests that you
        have been using your position to exploit participants who are in vulnerable situations despite
        your knowledge of your obligations under the NDIS Act. For these reasons, I am concerned
        your breaches will continue to occur in future;

    •   I also hold concerns about your willingness to deliver substandard services to NDIS
        participants considering you and your child are also participants. This shows a distinct lack of
        empathy and care for people who share common challenges.




                                   NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission                                4
                                                 202
                                                                                                       T9.7

    •       The matters set out above demonstrates conduct that falls well below the standard and
            quality of care expected from key personnel of NDIS providers when providing supports or
            services to people with disability. The information before me in relation to the protracted
            length of time over which the alleged conduct took place suggests that your conduct was
            systemic in nature. Allowing you to continue providing supports or services to people with
            disability poses a continuing risk to people with disability.

For the avoidance of doubt, the proposed Banning Order will prevent you from having any
involvement whether directly or indirectly in the delivery, coordination and management of NDIS
funded supports and services to people with disability. The ban will therefore preclude you from
remaining a director and shareholder of Forward Focused Group while it remains an NDIS provider or
have any involvement in any other entity that provides supports and services to NDIS participants. For
example, the banning order will also preclude you from remaining as one of the listed officeholders of
the Hive Pty Ltd, Hybrid Hive Pty Ltd, Infinity Living Options Pty Ltd, Malibuhouse Pty Ltd or Exact
Financial Solutions Pty Ltd if they provide any supports or services to people with disability.

NDIS Provider Register

Section 73ZS of the NDIS Act requires the Commissioner to maintain the NDIS Provider Register which
includes certain information about NDIS providers and other persons.

Under section 73ZS(2A) of the NDIS Act, the NDIS Provider Register must include certain information
in relation to a person against whom a Banning Order is in force. The information that must be
included on the NDIS Provider Register is:

        •    The name of the person
        •    The person’s ABN (if any); and
        •    Information about the Banning Order.

Sections 17 and 18 of the National Disability Insurance Scheme (Provider Registration and Practice
Standards) Rules 2018 provides that the whole of the NDIS Provider Register may be published on the
NDIS Commission’s website unless:

        •    the Commissioner considers that the publication of part of the NDIS Provider Register would
             be contrary to the public interest; or

        •    the Commissioner considers that the publication of part of the NDIS Provider Register would
             be contrary to the interests of one or more persons with disability receiving supports or
             services.

If I decide to make a Banning Order against you, information about the Banning Order will be included
on the NDIS Provider Register and published on the NDIS Commission’s website.

Making a submission

I have not made a final decision in respect of this matter and I will not do so until you have had an
opportunity to make submissions in writing to me. This gives you the opportunity to tell me anything
you believe may be appropriate to bring to my attention, to dispute any of the facts of this matter or
to comment on the seriousness of it.




                                     NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission                           5
                                                   203
                                                                                                        T9.7

Section 73ZN(7) of the NDIS Act provides that the Commissioner may only make a banning order
against a person after giving the person an opportunity to make submissions to the Commissioner on
the matter.

Any submissions you wish to make must be received by the Commission within 28 days of your receipt
of this notice.

You will be notified in writing of any decision made in relation to the proposed revocation and banning
order.

Should you wish to make a submission about the proposed Banning Order against you, please do so
in writing and either:

    •   Deliver it to:

                  NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission
                  Level 1, 200 St Georges Terrace, Perth WA 6000
                  Marked for the attention of:
                  Compliance Western Australia

    •   email it to wacompliance@ndiscommission.gov.au or
    •   send it by post addressed to:

                  Compliance Western Australia
                  NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission
                  Level 1, 200 St Georges Terrace, Perth WA 6000

In order to have any written submission considered in my decision-making please ensure that the
submission is sent to the email address, delivered to the street address or arrives at the postal address
as set above, by no later than 5 pm (AWST) on 16 November 2022.

If a submission is not received from you by this time, I will proceed to make my final decision.
However, you may wish to seek legal or other professional advice before deciding not to make a
submission.

Yours sincerely




Philip Halton
Branch Head, Operations - North Central West
NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission

Annexure A

NATIONAL DISABILITY INSURANCE SCHEME ACT 2013 - SECT 73ZN

Banning orders—NDIS providers

       (1) The Commissioner may, by written notice, make an order (a banning order) prohibiting
       or restricting specified activities by an NDIS provider, or by a person who was an NDIS
       provider, if:

           (a) the Commissioner has revoked the registration of the person as a registered NDIS
           provider; or

          (b) the Commissioner reasonably believes that:

                (i)     the person has contravened, is contravening, or is likely to contravene this
                        Act; or
                (ii)    the person has been involved in, or is likely to become involved in, a
                        contravention of this Act by another person; or
                (iii)    the person is not suitable to provide supports or services to people with
                        disability; or
                (iv)    in the case of an NDIS provider—there is an immediate danger to the
                        health, safety or wellbeing of a person with disability if the person
                        continues to be an NDIS provider; or

          (c) the person is convicted of an offence involving fraud or dishonesty; or

          (d) the person becomes an insolvent under administration.

           Note:      A person who is subject to a banning order cannot be registered as an NDIS
           provider in a way that is inconsistent with the order (see subsection 73E(3)).

Banning orders—persons employed or otherwise engaged by NDIS providers

      (2) The Commissioner may, by written notice, make an order (a banning order) prohibiting or
           restricting a person who is or was employed or otherwise engaged by an NDIS provider,
           or a person who is or was a member of the key personnel of an NDIS provider, from
           engaging in specified activities if:


          (a) the Commissioner reasonably believes that:
               (i) the person has contravened, is contravening, or is likely to contravene this Act; or
               (ii) the person has been involved in, or is likely to become involved in, a
                       contravention of this Act by another person; or
              (iii) the person is not suitable to be involved in the provision of supports or services
                       to people with disability; or
              (iv) there is an immediate danger to the health, safety or wellbeing of a person with
                       disability if the person continues to engage in the specified activities; or




                                NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission                               7
                                              205
                                                                                                    T9.7

            (b) the person is convicted of an offence involving fraud or dishonesty; or
            (c) the person becomes an insolvent under administration.

Application of banning orders

          (3) A banning order may:

                            (a) apply generally or may be of limited application; and

                            (b) be permanent or for a specified period.

       (4) However, a banning order cannot prohibit or restrict activities that a registered NDIS
            provider is registered to provide under 73E.

       (5) A banning order takes effect from the day specified in the order.

       (6) A banning order is not a legislative instrument.

Process

       (7) The Commissioner may only make a banning order against a person after giving the
            person an opportunity to make submissions to the Commissioner on the matter.

       (8) However, subsection (7) does not apply if the Commissioner’s grounds for making the
            banning order are, or include, the following:
           (a) that there is an immediate danger to the health, safety or wellbeing of a person with
                  disability;
           (b) that the Commissioner has revoked the registration of the person as a registered
                  NDIS provider.

       (9) The Commissioner must:
           (a) give the banning order to the person against whom the order is made; and
           (b) include in the banning order a statement of reasons for the order; and
           (c) if the banning order is against a person who is employed or otherwise engaged by an
                   NDIS provider—notify the NDIS provider of the order as soon as possible.

Civil penalty

      (10) A person contravenes this subsection if:
           (a) the person engages in conduct; and
           (b) the conduct breaches a banning order that has been made against the person.

                Civil Penalty:    1,000 penalty units.

Post-legal advice considerations of Evidence, information and considerations.

1. Summary of tip-Offs
   1.1 The NDIA received fifty-seven tip-offs between 3 February 2021 and 16 August
       2022, alleging:
      a. Serious allegations including intimidating and provoking participants.
      b. Claiming for services not provided.
      c. Directing staff to create false invoices.
      d. Overcharging for services.
      e. Misuse of funds – claiming for services from incorrect line items and supports
         not reasonable and necessary.
      f. Forgery – cutting and pasting participant/nominee signature onto service.
         agreements and forgery of a signature
      g. Bullying and harassment of participants and staff.
      h. Exhausting funds from expired plans.
      i. Participant neglect – required supports were not provided.

2. Company structure
   2.1 In an email dated 31 May 2022 (Att 15) the Founding Executive Director of
       Forward Focused Group advised that at the end of 2021 the executive team
       comprised of “Simon, Rejaul and I”. It includes information about new staff being
       brought in and an attached file to show the company future.
   2.2 The email is issued to “ffg.net.au and hybridhive.com.au” email addresses.
   2.3 The attached file (Att 16) shows Parent Company “Forward Focused Group” with
       linking arrows to four sub groups including: Forward Focused with I [heart] NDIS
       logo, and The Hive Support Services with “The Hive” logo.
   2.4 Based on this and additional information provided by staff about access to
       accounts of both Forward Focused and The Hive it is reasonable to conclude
       that the entities the Commission knows as Forward Focused Group Pty Ltd (Att
       17), Hybrid Hive Pty Ltd (Att 18) form part of “Forward Focused Group”.
   2.5 The news section of the Forward Focused Group website includes a note about
       the expansion into employment services under the banner of “Workforce
       Australia Employment Services” which is included on the company subgroups
       (Att 16)
   2.6 The Forward Focused Group website links to a Facebook page (Att 33).
   2.7 Service Agreements for participant and provider “Forward Focused Group”
       (Att 41 and Att 46) includes no ABN, ACN or registration number.
   2.8 Emails of 23 June 2022 shows email from Teams Coordinator @hybridhive
       email address to both @hybridhive.com.au and @ffg.net.au staff including
       Rejaul Alam and Vida Reid.

   Given the information presented it is reasonable to conclude that Forward Focused
   Group (FFG) is a catch all term for the companies which include the known entities
   referred to as Forward Focused and The Hive.


3. Companies interrelated
The information available both publicly and provided to the Commission demonstrates
that Forward Focused Group (FFG) is a name-only entity led by Vida Reid, Simon Reid
and Rejaul Alam. The name-only entity has a number of businesses including the NDIS
registered Forward Focused Group Pty Ltd also known as “Forward Focused”, and the
non-registered entity Hybrid Hive also known as The Hive.

        delivered by the Hive. It also references hearing a phone conversation on
        speaker between the support worker and Jess (H.R. Dept) (most likely FFG
        People and Culture, Att 23). It comments that the call was threatening
        including legal threats and repayment of pay.
   10.1.2      The participant also seeks to meet the following day to discuss the
        concerns.
   10.1.3      Although not in the original list of recipients there is a reply to the
        email from the Founding Executive Director, Vida Reid on 16 June 2022.
   10.1.4      The reply states that the support worker has a non-compete and non-
        disclosure contract which will prevent her for working privately for the
        participant. It also states that if the participant had “come to us in good
        faith…I would have said okay.”

        support worker to assist the participant.

        of $4000.

        coordination and terminate the contract for all services with immediate
        effect. The email claims “ongoing abusive manner of our staff by yourself

        immediately or be reported to the NDIS.

                                        Statement
                                       By Danica Hall

        Forward Focused Group [FFG] (ABN 98 643 878 639) is owned and operated by
married couple, Vida and Simon Reid. This is in partnership with Rejaul Alam, who is also the
Chief Financial Officer [CFO]. Vida and Simon also own ‘sister company’ the Hybrid Hive, or
‘The Hive (ABN 96 651 140 108). Vida is named as the Director of FFG while Simon is named
as the Director of The Hive, however, in reality, they both contribute to both companies and
Vida is the main one running both companies, with Simon being an absent boss most of the
time. In addition to this, there is progress happening towards starting a Plan Management
company called Exact Financial Solutions (ABN 50 656 716 497) (refer to folder ‘1 Exact
Financial Solutions’). This is to be run by Rejaul. So, three different companies; three
different people, but all connected and benefitting (refer to folder ‘2 Companies’).

        I first started at FFG in December 2021 as an Accounts Officer. I was brought into the
role with very limited experience in NDIS and no qualifications or training in accounting, and
that’s a consistent trend I’ve noticed; they hire people with little knowledge and
understanding of the NDIS and limited relevant qualifications, seemingly and evidently to
allow Vida to mould / groom them and train them up with what they want us to know and
do. The few staff that have been employed with experience and / or qualifications, don’t
last very long in their employment once it becomes evident that they are getting too close
to the truth of what is happening in these companies. They tend to just miraculously
disappear from the workplace. A good example of this was Jan Nuttal who was employed as
FFG’s Operations Manager. She was there for approximately 9 weeks, before being fired on
29/06/2022.

         Although coming in on an extremely low wage, I was just glad to have a job in what
at first appeared to be with an exciting, growing, fun company. However, things started to
come to light when early on my employment (just a couple of weeks in), three staff were
fired / resigned all on the same day (Grant and Melissa Williams and Toby). Since I started
my employment with FFG, I have seen 24 staff leave:
     • Grant Williams (fired - 16/12/2021).
     • Toby (resigned - 16/12/2021).
     • Melissa Williams (resigned, but would have been fired - 16/12/2021).
     • Emma Imani.
     • Rachael Thomas (resigned).
     • Shadi Norouzi (resigned).
     • Rebecca Bowers (resigned, but was going to be fired - 08/03/2022).
     • Hassan Zia (resigned - 16/03/2022, after 2 days).
     • Nakita Toia (resigned - 28/03/2022).
     • Elanor Smith (resigned - 29/03/2022).
     • Sally See (resigned - 05/04/2022).
     • Jaimi Lee Smith (fired - 06/04/2022).
     • Roshni Kaur (resigned - 26/04/2022).
     • Cori (resigned - 26/04/2022, after 3 days).
     • Brodie Hall (resigned - 06/05/2022).



                                              1

                                           225
                                                                                            T9.11

    • Ally Oleksiuk (resigned).
    • Blesson Varghese (resigned - 23/06/2022).
    • Jan Nuttal (fired / resigned - 29/06/2022).
    • Louise (resigned).
    • Tanya Gukwa (resigned - 05/07/2022).
    • Nassam (resigned - 05/07/2022, after a week).
    • Brooke (fired - 11/07/2022).
    • Sarah King (resigned - 15/07/2022).
    • Jodie Salmon (resigned - 15/07/2022).
For most of these people, they’ve disappeared without any explanation, leaving the
remaining staff to just work out that they’ve left or find out from another employee. On few
occasions, we have been informed, but with most of those, the person has been spoken
about negatively. The high turnover of staff is alone an indication of the kind of company
they are running.

        The next red flag was the New Year’s cruise. Something didn’t quite add up, but I put
it down to my lack of knowledge of the NDIS, however when I heard that we were charging
$7,500 per person, something didn’t sound right. I wasn’t too involved with this as I was still
fairly new and it didn’t make sense to me so I left it to the other Accounts Officer, Virginie
Fanny-Dulloo, to handle it while I focused on the other parts of our role. I was still aware of
what was happening and the conversations happening in the office - Support Coordinators
were asked on several occasions to just ‘call all of the participants’ to see who wanted to go,
even though participants had already been asked before (and declined), while also being
charged for each call - Participants didn’t seem to want to use $7,500 of their plan on a one-
night event (understandably) so Vida and Rejaul were scrambling to get numbers and were
pressuring staff to, in my opinion, harass participants into coming.

        Following a two week break over Christmas, I returned and this was when I started
to see things a lot more clearly. With the number of staff coming and going and then seeing
and hearing certain conversations and behaviours, I started to see that while most of us
were in this job for the participants, the people running these companies were just there to
make money for their own financial gain, and weren’t there to support the participants. As
it was, Vida and Simon were both coming home with a $140,000 salary and Rejaul was on
$100,000 per annum - Not bad for a company that was less than a year old. In my time at
FFG, I’ve seen participants get discarded once they can no longer steal their money (e.g.
Andrew Hahn, Madeline Robinson, Suzanne Shepherd, Alexsis Bott, Corina Abraham and
Sarah-Jane Clulow - All participants who are no longer with FFG / The Hive).

        In my time at FFG, I have been disgusted and appalled at the way I’ve seen Vida treat
staff and participants. Her unprofessionalism in the workplace around how she behaves is
unlike anything I’ve seen before. This include: Swearing, laughing hysterically, disclosing
unnecessary private information about other staff and participants and making fun of
certain situations, which I’ll discuss later. I’ve heard Vida make phone calls to our

Financial Abuse

Since starting at FFG as one of the Accounts Office, I noticed early on that questionable
invoices were being sent out for services that were never provided. Many of these invoices
have been for excessive amounts:
    1. Some from participants’ current plans, despite services not being provided, and;
    2. Others from participants old plans that have remaining funds available.
These are a complete misuse of NDIS funding and financial abuse, especially if it means that
participants are then left with no funding to use on services they actually need. False
information is being told to the NDIS in order for participants to get new plans so that FFG /
The Hive can then take the remaining funding from the previous plan. An example of this is

The Company - Systems and Processes / Policies & Procedures / Work Environment /
Bullying / Harassment

Other issues, more on a company level, include:
   • The structure is confusing around who’s in charge, and this causes inconsistencies,
       confusion and changes. Vida is too controlling and doesn’t have trust in her staff so
       gets involved in all aspects of the company, meaning people are undermined and
       micromanaged.
   • There are very limited systems and processes in place, impacting on efficiency and
       consistency of work and a lack of clarity around what and how something should be
       done. I have had to implement and put into place my own guides and manuals for
       Accounts.
   • There is far too much expectation on us with very little guidance; based on our role
       titles and pay levels, we are being asked to do things that are outside of our roles
       (e.g. For myself and Accounts, applying for grants we aren’t even eligible for and
       assisting with the audit process. We also have unrealistic deadlines that are then
       replaced with other deadlines with no understanding of which deadline now needs
       to be completed first).
   • Despite having three staff in Human Resources, some of the staff being hired are
       inefficient, unqualified and simply unable to do the job.
            o Some staff aren’t clear on their roles and there is minimal to no training
                provided.
            o Some staff don’t even have Job Descriptions for their roles (e.g. Janine Hall as
                the Service Manager and me as the Accounts Manager).
            o They are not following the Award (particularly around the payment of
                kilometres) and there are pay inconsistencies (e.g. As the Accounts Manager,
                I was on $5,000 less than one of the new Accounts Officers that started.
                We’ve also recently just had another Accounts Officer start who is only on
                $55,000).
            o There are different rules for different people and different expectations;
                some staff are ‘protected species’ and rules don’t apply to them.
   • Communication is poor. There’s been many times where I haven’t had replies or
       responses to an email or query and had to follow it up (usually more than once). I’ve
       also seen emails and queries from other stakeholders not get a response.
            o Depending on who you ask and sometimes when you ask, responses and
                decisions change or are simply inconsistent; we may be told one thing one
                day and then a different thing the next day. This is also due to the structure
                and there being ‘too many cooks in the kitchen’.
While these have more of an impact on the staff, there are some concerns around safety
when there are no systems and processes or Policies & Procedures and unqualified staff are
being employed and used.

Dear Mr Hahn

Re: Residence at 25/19 Malibu Road, Safety Bay

We refer to the above referenced property and confirm we act on behalf of Forward Focused
Group Pty Ltd on instructions from Ms Vida Reid.

On 21 January 2022 you entered into a lodging agreement with our client, to occupy
premises located at 25/19 Malibu Road, Safety Bay.

The lodging agreement is part of the services that, as a NDIS provider, our client provides to
eligible participants.

The fortnightly lodging fee is totalled at $332.08.

The terms of the agreement provide that you are required to pay a fortnightly accommodation
fee of $262.08 and a utility fee of $70.00. Therefore, the total fee payable each fortnight is
$332.08.

Under clause 17.3 of the agreement, our client maintains the right to terminate the
agreement in a number of circumstances, including, but not limited to –

(a)    The condition of the participant changes to the extent that the participant no longer
       needs the providers services, or their needs can be met more appropriately by other
       services or care.

(b)    The participants needs can not me met at the premises.




              Liability Limited by a scheme approved under Professional Standards Legislation
                                              239
                                                                                                T9.12

(c)    The participant cannot be cared or within the services due to unmanageable
       occupational safety and health risk, which puts Forward Focused Group staff and / or
       Service providers at high/extreme risk of injury or harm to health. This may include
       non-compliance with safety equipment and or safe work practices.

(d)    The participant breached policies and procedures and accommodation rules.

(e)    …

Further, clause 17.4 provides that our client may terminate the agreement immediately and
without notice in cases of “serious breaches” which include.

(f)    The participant fails to pay any accommodation fees within fourteen (14) days of due
       date for any reason within the participants control.

(g)    The participant causes or threatens serious injury to a member of staff or another
       housemate.

(h)    The participant causes or threatens damage to the premises or any other property at
       the premises.

(i)    Should there be a restraining order in place affecting and involving other occupants
       and / or support staff.


We are instructed that on entering in the agreement with our client and taking up residence
at the property you informed Ms Vida Reid that you were not going to pay the fees, as you
preferred to spend the funds received through the NDIS on “other things”.

On being informed by Ms Reid that your funding was, firstly, subject to being utilised for your
care and living expenses, and secondly, that you had agreed to be bound by the terms of the
agreement, you commenced acting in an antisocial manner engaging in such conduct as
follows –

1.     verbally abusing people who attend the premises;

2.     verbally abusing your fellow lodgers;

3.     punching tables and other items within the premises; and

4.     falsely informing vulnerable residents that our client is acting contrary to their
       interests for your own means.

On 30 March 2022, your actions resulted in emergency services being called to the
premises, and a distressed resident requiring immediate assistance.

A number of people were adversely affected as a result of your conduct, including our client’s
employee, who was in attendance with her 6-month-old child, and your fellow boarders.

Unfortunately, your actions amount to serious misconduct, and you have breached the terms
of the agreement, and continue to breach the terms of the agreement, by failing to pay the
fortnightly lodging fee.

Dear Ms Jonker

Re: Forward Focused Group; Incident 12 August 2022 and 17 August 2022

We confirm we have been retained by Forward Focused Group, on instructions from Ms Vida Reid.,
and have to hand your correspondence dated 16 August 2022.

A Meghan Goodwin has been a recipient of our client’s services since December 2020, including
supported independent living at one of our client’s properties in Safety Bay.

Ms Vida Reid holds the current appointment as Meghan’s legal plan nominee and next of kin, and we
enclose the relevant documentation for your review.

We are instructed that on Friday 12 August 2022, Meghan was covertly removed from the property
by an ex-employee of our client, who was posing as a support worker. As correctly stated in your
correspondence, Meghan is a vulnerable person, and our client, being responsible at the time for
Meghan, was understandably concerned for her welfare, and contacted the police.

Shortly after Meghan was covertly removed from her accommodation, our client lost all access to
Meghan’s plan management portal and received an email from Meghan’s personal email account,
which stated that she was ceasing all services from our client and their associated company, The
Hive.

Our client then received correspondence from a Ms Sukhi Tear, of Diversitas WA, who informed our
client that she was Meghan’s current support coordinator.

Prior to Meghan’s removal from her accommodation, our client had not been informed by your
client, or the relevant agencies, as to any changes with respect to her care services or provider. As we




                 Liability Limited by a scheme approved under Professional Standards Legislation
                                                242
                                                                                                    T9.13

are sure you will appreciate, there is a process to be followed, and said process does not justify the
covert removal of a vulnerable person from safe accommodation at night.

Whether or not Meghan has freely elected to cease receiving services with our client is irrelevant.
Correct procedure was not followed, and at the time Meghan was taken from her supported
independent living coordination, she was under the care and support of our client. Consequently, it is
our client that, at the time of Meghan’s covert removal, held a duty of care for Meghan, and was
therefore responsible for her safety and wellbeing.

Our client wholly rejects that they have harassed or pressured Meghan to return to them. We are
instructed that Meghan has directly contacted Ms Vida Reid several times since her removal and has
informed her that she is scared and that she does not know where she is or what is happening.

Our client takes no issue with participants electing to move to another service provider. What they
take issue with is the proper channels of communication being bypassed.

Participants are not “clients”. They are vulnerable people in need, receiving a service, and due care
and process must be taken with respect to them, and the supports they receive. In particular,
Meghan, having a highly psychosocial support need, should not have been removed in the manner
she was.

Further, as the appropriate channels of communication have been disregarded by your client and
other involved parties, there is no way of knowing whether Meghan has left of her own accord, and
whether she herself sent the email requesting our client cease their services.

No relevant documentation or authorities have been provided to confirm the change in Meghan’s
services and provider. Given the absence of such, our client naturally holds grave concerns for
Meghan’s wellbeing.

Communication regarding any changes with respect to Meghan’s care services or provider should
have been provided to our client, with proper procedure followed, prior to Meghan being removed
from our client’s care.

Unfortunately, this is now the third time your client has removed participants from our client without
any communication. It is disappointing that your client continually disregards correct procedure and
we put your client on notice that we have informed the NDIS of same.

We are further instructed that earlier today, one of our client’s support workers, Madeline Dawson,
was approached and physically assaulted by the director of your client, Amanda Damon.

At the time of the assault, Ms Dawson was in attendance with Mr Shapira, an elderly participant,
who has recently undergone heart surgery and is recovering from a stroke.

We are instructed that Ms Dawson has reported the incident to the police and provided a statement,
and that Mr Shapiera has lodged a formal complaint with the NDIS.

Given the recent events, we are instructed to request that your client, and any associated parties,
cease and desist approaching or otherwise communicating with our client’s employees, officers, or
participants.
